**Evaluating cooperative strategies**

I’m thinking about a policy for repeated interactions within a network of four neighbors. Since we have two cooperators and a low cooperation rate of 25%, I wonder if a Tit-for-Tat approach would be effective, mirroring my neighbors’ actions. Ultimately, balancing cooperation with exploitation is quite challenging.**Analyzing cooperation vs. defection**

But if I defect, I simply get 2b. Given that defecting always yields a greater net benefit—since cooperating is less effective—I'm leaning towards defecting in this situation. With a low overall cooperation rate in the network, the answer just seems to be zero for this round.

**Evaluating cooperation costs**

**Analyzing cooperation dynamics**

I'm exploring the idea of cooperation among neighbors and its implications. If neighbors don't cooperate, the reward for cooperating becomes tricky—it's tied to the number of neighbors and their benefits. Essentially, if I cooperate, I incur a cost for each neighbor without proportional gain to me. So, my payoff ends up being negative with cooperation, while defecting leads to a neutral payoff. It seems like the rational choice here is to defect.
